From:  Lucas  Workman  <lworkman@crusoe.ai> 

Sent:  Friday,  May  8,  2026  3:41  PM 

To: Gary Jungermann  <GJungermann@Callawaycounty.org>;  Callaway  Commission 

<comish@callawaycounty.org>;  Heather Tazalla  <htazalla@crusoe.ai>;  Conor  Bates-Janigo 

<cbatesjanigo@crusoe.ai> 

Subject: Tax Revenue  Estimates 

Hi  Callaway  County  Commissioners  - 

|  hope  you  all  had  a  nice  week.  Please  find  attached  the  updated  tax  revenue  estimates  broken  out  by 

each  taxing jurisdiction.  These  estimates  are  based  on  the  requested  terms:  an  85%  property tax 

abatement  for  a  25-year term,  assuming  the  Ambulance  and  Fire  District  opts  out  of the  Chapter  100 

abatement.  This  will  result  in  an  estimated  438%  annual  increase to  the  School  District's  revenue and  a 

31%  annual  increase  to  the  County's  revenue. 

Both  attached  files  contain  the  same  numbers, just formatted  differently. We  look forward  to 

discussing  this  proposal  in  further  detail  on  Monday  and  reviewing the  potential  community  benefit 

projects you  all  provide. 

Disclaimer: All tax  revenue  figures presented in  this proposal are  estimates  only and are  subject to  change 

depending  on  market  conditions,  the  requirements of the  end-user tenant,  the  final design  and  configuration  of the 

facility,  the  total capital investment deployed,  and the  application  of Callaway  County Assessor rules  and 

assessment practices. 

Thanks 

Lucas  Workman 

Manager, State  &  Local  Economic  Development 

Phone  Number:  (970)  302-1879

MAC's  bill  tracker/Weekly Wrap  Up 
Stay  informed  on  the  progress of  House  and  Senate  bills  being  monitored  by 
MAC  during  the  2026  Legislative  Session  and  listen  to  the  Weekly Wrap  Up. 

MAC’s  2026  Legislative  Bill  Tracker provides  a  comprehensive  overview of  bills 
as  they  move  through  the  legislative  process.  Visit  MAC’s website  regularly  to 
stay  up  to  date  on  legislative  developments  throughout  the  session. 

Click  to  view  the  bill  categories 

MAC's Weekly Wrap-Up  provides  comprehensive  coverage of  the  key  issues  and 

initiatives  that  are  currently  being  prioritized.  From  legislative  updates  and 

policy changes  to  important  news  and  events,  we  ensure  that  you  are  well- 

informed  about  the  matters  that  impact  our  counties  and  communities. 

Listen  to  the  episodes 

Missouri  News 

Missouri  House  passes  second  attempt  at  legislation  changing 

property tax  policies 
Missouri  House  members  have  passed  legislation  aimed  at  lowering  property 

taxes. 

Representatives  voted  133-13  to  pass  the  legislation.  It  now  goes  to  the 

Senate,  where  Republican  Senate  Floor  Leader Tony  Luetkemeyer, R- 

Parkville,  has  already  said  reducing  property  taxes  is  a  priority. 

Speaking  before  the  vote,  bill  sponsor  Rep.  Tim  Taylor, R-Bunceton,  praised 
the  legislation  and  the  work  of  the  special  committee  he  chaired  on  property 

taxes. 

“If this  passes  this  final journey,  we  can  assure  our  constituents  that  we  have 
made  their  lives  better, and,  quite  frankly,  made  our  lives  better  as 

taxpayers  as  well,  in  stabilizing  the  current  system  so  that  we  can  continue 

to work forward  in  the years  to  come  to  make  it even  better,” Taylor said. 
Read  more 

Judge  revises  ballot  language  for  plan  to  make  it  harder to  amend  Missouri  Constitution 
A  ballot  measure  pushed  by  Republicans  to  change  how  majorities  are  calculated  in  Missouri  for 
constitutional  amendments  proposed  by  initiative  must  clearly  alert  voters  that  is  its  intent,  a  Cole 
County  judge  ruled  Wednesday.  Read  more 

Foes  of  new Missouri  congressional  map  say they have  signatures for  a  November vote 
A group  aiming  to  place  a  new Missouri  congressional  map  up  for  a  statewide vote  says  it will  have  enough 
signatures  for  a  November  referendum.  Read  more 

Republicans  push  forward  with  proposal  to  replace Missouri  income  tax with  sales  tax 

The  proposal  to  eliminate  the  state  income  tax  is  moving  to  the  Missouri  House  floor  following  a  party- 

line  committee  vote  Wednesday.  Read  more 

Missouri  lawmakers  weigh  $294M  price  tag  to  carry  out  new federal Medicaid  rules 
Missouri  lawmakers Wednesday  once  again  discussed  a  request  for  $294.6  million  —  including  $35  million 

in  state  general  revenue  —  to  help  the  state  implement  federally  mandated  changes  to  safety  net 

programs.  Read  more 

Shopping  around  for  power:  Missouri  bill  aims  to  break  up  electric  utilities 

Don  Mayhew  believes  in  the  free  market.  “We  have  choice  in  a  lot  of  different  things.  Go  to  a  grocery 

store  —  there’s four  different  brands  of  green  beans  you  can  buy,”  said  Mayhew,  a  Republican  Crocker. 

“We  know  that  competition  in  any  marketplace  is  the  foundation  —  the  cornerstone  —  of  our  capitalist 

system.”  Read  more 

Missouri  lawmakers  told  cost  is  unknown  to fix  problem-plagued  financial  system 

A Missouri  state  accounting  system  that  was  supposed  to  integrate  all  state  financial  operations  on  one 

platform  is  late  and  over  budget  —  and  the  program’s top  executive can’t  say  when  it  will  be  working  or 

how  much  more  it will  cost.  Read  more 

Bill  to  create  agriculture  education  program  gets  unanimous  support  from  Missouri  House 

Hoping  to  grow  the  next  generation  of  farmers,  the  Missouri  House  unanimously  approved a  bill  Monday  to 

create  an  optional  agriculture  education  program  for  elementary  schools.  Read  more 

County  News 

‘Blinded  by dollar  signs’:  Independence  wrestles with 
90%  tax abatement  for  massive Al  data  center 
Rachel  Gonzalez  did  not  foresee  becoming  an  opposition  leader  to 
what  could  be  the  largest  economic  development  project  in  the 
history  of  Independence,  Missouri. 

She  is  a  lifelong  resident,  living just  a  few miles  from  a  proposed 
hyperscale  data  center  site.  She  knows  and  voted  for  several  of  the 
council  members  who  will  decide  whether  to  give  tax  abatements 
to  the  project  —  a  vote  that  may  ultimately  decide  the  fate  of  the 
“Al factory.” 

When  she  first  heard  about  the  data  center  late  last  year, she 
assumed  the  more  she  learned,  the  better  she  would  feel  about  it. 

The  opposite  happened.  Read  more 

Property tax  caps  on  April  ballot  in  several  counties  worry  districts 

At  the April  7  election,  voters  in  several  southwest  Missouri  counties  will  be  asked  to  weigh  in  on  property 

tax  caps  created  by state  legislators.  As  assessments  and  property  tax  bills  have  balloons,  limiting  how 

much  property  taxes  can  grow in  the  future  could  be  enticing,  but  the  constitutionality  of  the  legislation 

is  yet  to  be  decided  and  the  tax  freezes  could  have  significant  effects  on  the  services  property  taxes 

fund.  Read  more 

Boone  County  considers  ending  renewal  requirement for  senior  property tax  program 

The  Boone  County  Commission  is  considering  an  amendment  to  the  county's  senior  real  estate  tax 
program  policy  that  would  remove the  requirement for  annual  renewal.  Read  more 

Sam  Page  asks for  $8.6M for  St.  Louis  County  budget for jails,  roads  and  police 
St.  Louis  County  Executive  Sam  Page  said  the  county  should  add  back  $8.6  million  of expenses  into  its 
tight  budget.  Read  more 

Crusoe  pitches  80%  personal  property tax abatement  for  data  center  project 
The  Warrenton  Board  of Aldermen  listened  to  a  proposal  from  Crusoe,  the  builder  of  the  proposed  data 
center  in  the  city,  for  an  80  percent  personal  property tax  abatement  during  a  work  session  Feb.  23.  Read 

more 

Festus  faces  second  lawsuit  over  data  centers 
A  Festus  resident  has  filed  a  lawsuit  against  the  city of  Festus,  asking  the  courts  to  reverse  recent City 

Council  actions  involving  data  centers.  Read  more 

Share  your  county's  achievements  and  honors 
The  Missouri  Association  of Counties  would  like  you  to  Show Me  Your 

County. 

Show Me Your County is a chance for our counties to highlight a 

program,  initiative  or  praise  a  hard-working  county  employee  who  has 
gone  above  and  beyond  their  duties  or  has  received  an  award. 

Counties can  submit a  500-word or  less description  of the  program, 
initiative  or  employee,  along  with  pictures  if  applicable.  Those 
submissions  will  then  be  featured  on  MAC’s  social  media  platforms  and 

in  the  Missouri  County  Record  magazine. 

Submit  your  county  today

BLUE  SHIELD 

PROGRAM 
Communities Committed to

Governor  Kehoe  announces  opportunity for  more Missouri 

communities  to  earn  Blue  Shield  designation 
Governor Mike  Kehoe  announced  Missouri  counties,  towns,  and  cities  can  now 

apply  to  be  recognized  as  Blue  Shield  communities  in  2026  for  their  dedication 

to  effective  law  enforcement  and  community  safety. 

The  Missouri  Department  of  Public  Safety  administers  the  Blue  Shield  Program. 

Applications  should  be  made  by  an  ofÏcial  from  the  jurisdictions  seeking  the 

Blue  Shield  designation  in  coordination  with  the  jurisdiction’s chief  law 

i 

enforcement  ofÏcer. 

Deadline  to  submit  is  June  1,  2026.  Applications  are  made  online,  and  all 

supporting  materials  can  be  uploaded  here  .

Gary, 

Attached  are estimates  of real  property taxes for the  proposed  data  center.  One  big  uncertainty  is  the 

valuation  of the  data  center.  In  Montgomery  County, Amazon  estimated  a  value  equal  to  45%  of the  real 
property cost.  That same  valuation  was  used  in  Warrenton/Crusoe —  though |  don’t  recall  any  specific 
discussions  about that  point with  Crusoe or  its  lawyers  (Polsinelli).  For the  Montgomery  County/Google 
data  center, Google  (through  Polsinelli)  is  asking  us to  use  a  value  of $300/sf.  Travis thinks that  is 
ridiculously  low.  One  of my  partners  had  discussions with  the  Clay County Assessor regarding valuation 
of a  data  center in  Liberty,  and  the Assessor estimated  $436/sf. 

So bottom  line:  we’ve shown  all  three  scenarios;  as you  can  see,  they  produce very different results.

Hi  Callaway  County Commissioners  -

estimates are  based  on  the  requested terms:  an  85%  property tax  abatement for a  25-year term,  assuming  the Ambulance and  Fire  District

annual  increase to  the  County's  revenue.

County Assessor rules  and assessment practices.

CRUSOE  TECHNOLOGIES 

CONFIDENTIAL 

May  8th,  2026 

Crusoe 

Re:  Proposed  Chapter  100  Industrial  Development  Revenue  Bond  Agreement —  Crusoe 
Technologies Data  Center  Project,  Callaway  County,  Missouri 

Dear  Callaway  County  Commissioners: 

Crusoe  Technologies  LLC  (“Crusoe”)  is  pleased  to  present this  formal  request for  a  Chapter  100 
Industrial  Development  Revenue  Bond  (“Chapter  100”)  agreement  with  Callaway  County, 
Missouri.  Crusoe  is  a  technology  infrastructure  company  focused  on  purpose-built,  sustainable 
data  centers  and  high-performance  computing  facilities.  We  are  actively  evaluating  Callaway 
County  as  the  site  for  a  significant  capital  investment  that  would  bring  lasting  economic  and 
fiscal  benefits  to  the  region. 

We  respectfully  propose  the  following  terms  and  set  forth  below  our  substantive justifications  for 
those  terms,  along  with  a  summary  of the  projected  fiscal  impact  (“85%  PDF”). 

|.  PROPOSED  CHAPTER  100  TERMS 

Agreement Term: 25  years  from  the  date  of  project  completion. 

Abatement  Percentage:  85%  abatement  on  real  property  and  personal  property taxes 

assessed  against  the  project  during  the  agreement term. 

Property  Subject to  Abatement: All  real  property  comprising  the  data  center facility  and 

associated  on-site  improvements,  and  all  personal  property,  including  servers,  networking 

equipment,  and  other  qualifying  tangible  personal  property  placed  in  service  at  the  site. 

PILOT &  Community  Benefit  Payments:  Payments  in  Lieu  of Taxes (“PILOT”) and  Community 

Benefit  payments  will  be  structured  in  collaboration  with  the  taxing  jurisdictions  to  direct 

meaningful,  recurring  revenue to  the  community. 

Jobs Commitment:  Crusoe  shall  commit  to  creating  and  maintaining  an  agreed  upon  minimum 
number  of full-time  equivalent jobs  at  the  facility,  with  an  average  annual  wage  equal  to  or 
exceeding  150%  of the  average  annual  wage  in  Callaway  County,  as  determined  by  reference 
to  applicable  Missouri  Department  of  Economic  Development  data. 

Fees:  Crusoe  shall  be  responsible  for  reasonable  and  documented  legal  fees  and  expenses  of 
the  County  related  to  the  Project,  including  Gilmore  &  Bell,  P.C., bond  counsel  to  the  County, 
and  the  selected  bond  trustee. 

ll.  PROJECTED  FISCAL  IMPACT 

The  following  table  summarizes  the  projected  tax  revenue  to  local  taxing  jurisdictions,  based  on 
the  estimated  assessed  value  of  the  project.  These  figures  will  flow  directly  to  local  taxing 
jurisdictions  each  year,  plus  any  Community  Benefit  payments  negotiated  as  part  of the  Chapter 
100  structure.  The  figures  presented  below  are  preliminary,  illustrative  estimates  only  and  do  not 
constitute  a  representation,  warranty,  or  commitment  by  Crusoe  as  to  actual  tax  revenue  that 
will  be  generated  by  the  project.  These  estimates  are  based  on  assumptions  regarding  capital 
investment,  facility  configuration,  end-user  tenant  requirements,  and  assessment  methodology, 
any  of  which  may  change.  Actual  results  may  differ  materially.  Please  review  the  disclaimer 

following  the  table  for  additional  detail.

Disclaimer:  All  tax  revenue  figures  presented  in  this  proposal  are  estimates  only  and  are  subject  to 
change  depending  on  market  conditions,  the  requirements  of  the  end-user  tenant,  the  final  design  and 
configuration  of  the  facility,  the  total  capital  investment  deployed,  and  the  application  of Callaway  County 
Assessor rules  and  assessment practices. 

lll.  JUSTIFICATION  FOR  PROPOSED  TERMS 

Crusoe  respectfully  submits  that  the  proposed  25-year  term  and  85%  abatement  are 

well-supported  by  the  specific  economic  and  development  characteristics  of this  project  and  the 

Callaway  County  market.  The  following  factors,  taken  together, distinguish  this  project from  sites 

in  competing  jurisdictions: 

1.  Higher  Property Tax Rate  Than  Comparable  Sites 

Callaway  County's  effective  property  tax  rate  on  commercial  and  industrial  real  and  personal 

property 

is  materially  higher 

than 

that  of  comparable  data  center  sites  under  active 

consideration  in  Missouri  and  the  nation.  The  85%  abatement  corrects  for  this  structural  cost 

disparity  and  places  the  County  on  an  equal  footing  with  competing  sites. 

2.  Hyperscaler Tenant Requirements  &  Site  Competitiveness 

is  pursuing  a  hyperscaler  tenant  for  this  facility.  The  tenant  will  be  a  large-scale, 
Crusoe 
credit-worthy  technology  company  with  the  financial  capacity  to  make  substantial  ongoing 
investments  in  GPU  and  computing  infrastructure  at  the  site.  Hyperscaler tenants  operate  under 

internal 

rigorous 
a 
total-cost-of-ownership  basis.  An  abatement  structure  that  keeps  the  all-in  occupancy  cost 
competitive  is  often  a  threshold  requirement  for  site  selection.  Securing  such  a  tenant  is  critical 

frameworks  and  evaluate 

return-on-capital 

competing 

sites  on 

not  only  to  the  viability  of  this  project,  but  to  maximizing  the  long-term  capital  investment  and 

associated  economic  activity  in  Callaway  County. 

3.  Higher  Utility  &  Transmission Costs 

Data  center  operations  are  energy-intensive.  The  utility  and  transmission  cost  structure  for  this 
prospective  project  is  higher  than  in  certain  competing  markets,  adding  meaningful  operating 
cost  pressure  to  the  project  on  an  annual  basis. 

4.  Higher  Public  Improvement  Costs 

Development  of  this  site  will  require  significant  public  infrastructure  improvements.  These 
include  road  improvements  to  accommodate  the  construction  trafÏc  and  ongoing  operational 
logistics  associated  with  a  data  center  campus,  as  well  as  a  necessary  waterline  upgrade  to 
ensure  adequate  water  supply  and  pressure  for  cooling  and  fire  suppression  systems.  These 
capital  costs,  borne  directly  by  Crusoe,  represent a  real  and  material  cost to  develop the  project. 

Crusoe  Energy  Systems  |  Chapter  100  Proposal  |  Callaway  County,  MissouriPage  2 

CRUSOE  TECHNOLOGIES 

CONFIDENTIAL 

Crusoe  = 

5.  Structuring  Payments  to  Keep  Money  Local 

The  Chapter  100  structure  affords  considerable  flexibility  in  how  PILOT and  Community  Benefit 
payments  are  allocated  and  directed.  Crusoe  is  committed  to  working  collaboratively  with 
Callaway  County  and  the  other  taxing  jurisdictions  to  design  a  payment  structure  that  is 
consistent  with  applicable  law  and  delivers  meaningful,  recurring  revenue  to  local  entities. 

6. 

Comparable  Data  Center  Chapter  100  Agreement terms  in  Missouri 

Crusoe  believes  the  proposed  abatement  terms  are  more  competitive  and  result  in  higher 

community  benefit  than  other  recently approved  Chapter  100  Bond  Agreements.  For  example: 

e  Nebius  — Independence,  Missouri  (Jackson  County)  Approved March  2026 

o  20-year  term  at  98%  abatement  for  both  real  and  90%  abatement  for  personal 

property  taxes 

e  Mica — Kansas  City,  Missouri  (Port  KC) Approved  in  July 2025 

o  25-year  term  with  a  75%  abatement  on  real  and  personal  property  taxes, 

complemented  by  a  100%  sales  tax  exemption  for  all  construction  materials  and 

project  equipment. 

IV.  NEXT  STEPS 

Crusoe  welcomes  the  opportunity  to  present  these  terms  in  greater  detail  to  the  Commission 
and  its  advisors  and  to  work  cooperatively  toward  a  mutually  beneficial  agreement.  We  are 
prepared  to  engage  directly  with  any  relevant  stakeholders  to  advance  this  process  and  work 

collaboratively  with  the  community. 

We  look  forward  to  a  continued  and  productive  dialogue  regarding  this  important  investment 
opportunity  for  Callaway  County.  Please  do  not  hesitate  to  contact  us  at  your  earliest 
convenience. 

Respectfully  submitted, 

Crusoe  Technologies LLC 

Lucas  Workman

Fw:  Now Accepting  Nominations:  Bruce  Harris  Excellence  in  Leadership Award 

From  Callaway  Commission  <comish@callawaycounty.org > 

Date  Tue  3/3/2026  10:36  AM 

To 

Commissioners  <commissioners@callawaycounty.org> 

From: Tamara Tateosian <tamara@callawaychamber.net> 

Sent:  Tuesday,  March  3,  2026  10:34 AM 

Subject:  Now Accepting  Nominations:  Bruce  Harris  Excellence  in  Leadership  Award 

Dear Chamber  Members, 

The  Callaway  Chamber of  Commerce  is  now  accepting  nominations  for the  Bruce  Harris  Excellence  in 

Leadership Award,  which  will  be  presented  at  the  Leadership  and  Junior  Leadership  Graduation  on  May 

14th  at  Legends  Rec-Plex. 

This  award  honors  the  legacy of  Bruce  Harris,  a  respected  business  leader  in  Callaway  County from 

1999-2012.  Bruce  exemplified  a  positive  attitude,  extraordinary character, and  servant  leadership.  He 

led  by  example,  was  easily  approachable,  and  was  known  for  bringing  community  leaders  together to 

solve  problems  and  move  Callaway  County forward. 

About  Bruce  Harris 

Bruce  Harris was a  friend,  a  leader, a  gentleman,  and  a  mentor to  many. 

A graduate  of  Fulton  High  School,  Bruce  earned  his  degree  in  Psychology from  Westminster College  in 

1976  before  beginning  his  36-year  career at  The  Callaway  Bank.  He  was  also  a  graduate  of the 

Graduate  School  of Banking  in  Madison,  Wisconsin. 

Throughout his  career, Bruce  actively  served  on  more  than  two  dozen  boards that  made  significant 

impacts  in  Callaway County.  His  many  honors  included: 

J.H. Atkinson Award  Recipient 

Fulton  Colleges  Board  of Associates Alumni Award 
Student  Government Association Alumni Award  —  Westminster College 

National Award  of  Merit —  Phi  Delta  Theta  International  Fraternity 

Fulton  Public  Schools  Outstanding  Service  in  Education Award 

“|  did not keep a  list of these  things  as  they were  numerous,  and |  did not agree  to  work on  those 
projects just to  build a  resume.  If |  did it,  |  did it because  |  believed |  could help,” Bruce  T.  Harris,  CEO, 
The  Callaway Bank 

We  invite  you  to  submit  nominations  for  individuals who  embody  Bruce’s spirit  of leadership,  integrity, 
and  dedication  to  community. 

Bruce  Harris Award  Nomination  Form: 

https://Ip.constantcontactpages.com/sv/NVLNWnl/excellenceinleadership 

Nominations  must  be  received  by  Friday, April  17" to  be  considered. 

We  look forward  to  recognizing  another outstanding  leader  in  our  community. 

Best  Regards, 

Tamara Tateosian,  |OM

City of Holts Summit Announces Retirement of City Administrator Brandon  Ruediger 

Holts Summit,  Missouri,  3/12/2026 —  After  more  than  six  years  of  dedicated  service  to the  City  of  Holts 

Summit,  City Administrator Brandon  Ruediger has announced  his  retirement from  public service,  effective 

March  15,  2026. 

Ruediger  joined  the  City of Holts  Summit  in  January of 2020  and  worked  closely with  the  Mayor, Board  of 

Aldermen,  city staff,  local  businesses,  and  residents to strengthen city operations,  improve infrastructure, 

and  guide  responsible community growth. 

During  his  tenure,  the  City  of  Holts  Summit  experienced  significant  progress  across  multiple  areas of city 

government.  Key  accomplishments  during  this  time  include  strengthening  the  city’s  financial  stability 

through  responsible  budgeting  and 

long-term  financial  planning,  investing  in  critical  infrastructure 

improvements,  enhancing  public  safety  operations  and  equipment,  modernizing  internal  administrative 

systems,  and  improving transparency and  communication  with  residents. 

Under  Ruediger’s  leadership,  the  city also  focused  on  improving operational  efÏciency  and  strengthening 

internal  policies to  better support employees and  ensure effective delivery of services to  residents.  Efforts 

were  made  to  modernize  technology  systems,  improve  fleet  and  facility  management  practices,  and 

expand  safety  and  risk  management  initiatives for city  departments. 

Ruediger  also  worked  to  strengthen  partnerships  throughout  the  region  and  support  the  continued 

growth  of  the  local  business  community.  By  fostering  collaboration  with  local  organizations,  business 

leaders,  and  community  groups,  the  city  continued  to  build  momentum  as  an  attractive  and  growing 

community within  Callaway County. 

Reflecting  on  his  time  with  the  city,  Ruediger  expressed  deep  appreciation  for  the  people  who  helped 

move the  community forward. 

“Serving  the  City  of  Holts  Summit  has  been  one  of  the  greatest  honors  of  my  professional  career,” said 

Ruediger. “|  want to  sincerely thank the  Mayor and  the  Board  of Aldermen  for their  leadership,  trust,  and 

support  throughout  the  years.  Their  commitment  to  this  community  has  played  a  major  role  in  the 

progress the  city  has  achieved.” 

Ruediger also  recognized  the  dedication  of city  employees who  serve the  community  every day. 

City Administrator Brandon  Ruediger  serves as  the  primary  point of contact for  all  municipal  affairs.  Stay  updated  with  the  latest 

news  and  announcements  by  visiting  our  at  https://www.holtssummit.gov/, 

following  us  on 

Facebook  at 

https://www.facebook.com/CityofHoltsSummitMo/, or by  downloading the  Holts Summit  Connect app  on  the Apple and  Google 

Play  stores. 

“Our  city  employees  are  truly  the  backbone  of  this  organization.  Their  professionalism,  work  ethic,  and 

pride  in  serving  the  public  make  Holts  Summit  a  better  place  to  live  and  work.  It  has  been  a  privilege  to 

work alongside  such  a  talented  and  committed  group of  public  servants.” 

He  also  acknowledged  the  role  of the  community  in  the  city’s  continued  success. 

“lam  incredibly  grateful  to  the  many  local  business  owners  and  citizens  who  care  deeply  about  this 

community  and  who  have  helped  guide  its  growth.  The  willingness  of  residents  to  stay  engaged  and 

support  their local  government  is  what  truly makes  Holts  Summit  special.” 

As  he  concludes  his  public  service  career, Ruediger  says  he  remains  proud  of the  work  accomplished  and 

confident  in  the  city’s future. 

“Holts  Summit  is  a  community  filled  with  opportunity,  strong  leadership,  and  people  who  genuinely  care 

about  their  city.  |  am  thankful  for  the  friendships  and  partnerships  built  during  my  time  here  and  look 

forward to  seeing the  community  continue  to  grow and  succeed  in  the  years  ahead.” 

Additional  information  regarding  new  City  Administrator  Nicholas  Tietsort  will  be  provided  in  a  separate 

press  release. 

HHH 

City Administrator Brandon  Ruediger  serves  as  the  primary  point of contact for all  municipal  affairs.  Stay  updated  with  the  latest 

news  and  announcements  by  visiting  our  at  https://www.holtssummit.gov/, 

following  us  on 

Facebook  at 

https://www.facebook.com/CityofHoltsSummitMo/, or  by downloading the  Holts Summit Connect app  on  the Apple and  Google 

Play  stores.

Please  see  the  message  below  from  MoDOT. 

Fact  Sheet 

The  Missouri  Department  of Transportation  (MoDOT)  is  in  the  process  of 
updating  its  current  Long-Range  Transportation  Plan  (LRTP)  and  State 
Freight and  Rail  Plan  (SFRP).  We  kindly  request that  you  share  this  survey 
with  your organization,  boards,  commissions,  and  colleagues.  Obtaining 
feedback from  a  variety  of perspectives  will  allow these  plans  to  more 
effectively  address  the  transportation  needs  of  Missouri.  We  appreciate  your 
support  and  participation. 

Attached  you  will  find  a  fact  sheet  for  the  LRTP  and  SFRP  updates  that  can 

also  be  shared.  Visit  the  Project Website  for  more  information. 

Draft  language to  share: 

The  Missouri  Department  of Transportation  (MoDOT)  is  in  the  process  of 

updating  its  current  Long-Range  Transportation  Plan  (LRTP) and  State 

Freight and  Rail  Plan  (SFRP).  We  are  soliciting  feedback  from  Missourians 

on  transportation  priorities  to  help  determine  the  goals  for  the  state’s 25-year 

transportation  vision.  We  value  your  insights  and  perspectives,  which  are 

crucial  in  shaping  the  future  of transportation  in  our  state. 

Make  your  voice  heard  and  take  the  survey  by April  30,  2025: 

Home  |  MoDOT Long-Range  Transportation  Plan  and  State  Freight  &  Rail 

Plan 

For  more  information,  visit  our  website:  Long-Range  Transportation  Plan  and 

State  Freight  and  Rail  Plan  Updates  |  Missouri  Department  of  Transportation

NuclearNews 
Daily 

In this issue:  More on  LLNL and  Inertia’s R&D  agreements,  a  tour of 

Chernobyl,  a  bill to  expand  DOE authority in the  Senate, 

Japan  and 

Bulgaria repository updates,  and  more. 

Throwback Thursday: This  Sunday will mark  114  years  since the birth 

of Glenn  Seaborg.  Today’s TBT will take  a  look at  Seaborg’s massive 

impact  on the very beginnings  of the  U.S.  nuclear industry. 

ea V/A 

Advanced  Lighting  Systems 

LLNL and  Inertia  sign  R&D  partnership 

agreements 

Yesterday, Lawrence Livermore National  Laboratory and  Inertia 

Enterprises announced a  strategic research and development partnership 

to  address fusion challenges including laser development, fusion target 

design,  and target fabrication technologies  on the path to  a  commercial 

power plant.

Griffolyn  Specialty  Plastics  in  the  Nuclear Industry 

Take preventative  measures to  ensure  systems  are  protected  from foreign material 

with  Griffolyn® tarps.  FME tarps  help  ensure that  machines  and  parts  that are 

sensitive  or valuable  do  not  get contaminated  or  damaged  by foreign  objects.

In  other  news... 

The Bureau of Land Management is  reviewing an  environmental 

assessment of the  proposed  Dewey-Burdock uranium mining project  in 

South  Dakota  and is  taking public  comment on  its  draft assessment 

through May 14.  E&E News 

The United Kingdom’s Atomic  Energy Authority has  released its 

2026—2030  organizational strategy for its  national  fusion  laboratory. 

Objectives  include bolstering exports,  developing a  new workforce,  and 

building new research facilities.  NIA  UK 

The cruise industry is  exploring nuclear power to  drive  a  sustainable 

and emissions-free fleet.  Before adopting nuclear-powered propulsion, 

remaining hurdles include international regulatory alignment and public 

perception. Lloyd’s Register 

CANES at MIT held a  symposium focused on  U.S. nuclear energy 

dominance.  It highlighted the  need for international partnerships, 

streamlined regulations,  and public rebranding. MIT 

In a  new exclusive, reporter Matthew Sparkes tours  Chernobyl, talking 

with scientists about how the  exclusion zone has  recovered, the  impact of 

the war on  the  site,  and the legacy it holds today. New Scientist 

(registration required) 

Bulgaria ofÏcially opened its  national  repository for low- and 

intermediate-level radioactive waste. The site has  a  total capacity of 

138,200  cubic meters.  OfÏcials expect for it to be  filled over the  next 60 

years.  World Nuclear News 

New York expects to  select  a  community to host at least  1  GW of new 

nuclear energy by the  end of the year. Bloomberg (subscription 

required) 

EPRI’s recent report on the applications of heavy beam welding for 

heavy section components  of various types  of reactors illustrates how the 

technology can be leveraged to  speed nuclear deployment. Power 

Magazine 

U.S. utilities plan to  invest  $1.4 trillion over the  next five years,  driven 

by the AI boom  and the  need to upgrade the  aging power grid.  Wall 

Street Journal (subscription required) 

The White House is investigating claims,  amid circulating conspiracy 

theories, that recent deaths  and disappearances of scientists who may 

have had access to  classified nuclear and aerospace materials might be 

connected.  The  Times 

senate  bill  looks  to  clarify  DOE 

authority  over advanced  reactors 

Sen.  Mike  Lee  (R.,  Utah),  chair of the  Senate  Committee  on  Energy and 

Natural  Resources  (ENR),  has  introduced a  bill that would grant the 

Department  of Energy greater authority over new nuclear projects  under 

the  federal  agency’s  oversight.

Rolls-Royce,  GBE-N  contract 
kickstarts  U.K.’s SMR  plans  for Wylfa 

site 

Ten months after Rolls-Royce SMR emerged as the  United  Kingdom’s 
preferred bidder to build the  U.K.’s first small modular reactors, the 
company and the  U.K. government’s Great British  Energy—Nuclear have 
signed  a  contract allowing work to begin  at the site  of the 
decommissioned Wylfa nuclear plant  in  North  Wales. 

Go  deeper on  Nuclear NewsWire.

Japan  to  survey  Pacific  island  for 
potential  HLW  repository 

Japan will study the  possibility of siting a  deep  geologic  repository for 

high-level  radioactive waste  on the  remote  island  of Minamitorishima, 

about  1,200  miles southeast  of Tokyo.

Integrating  Waste  Management for 
Advanced  Reactors:  The  Universal 
Canister System  and  Project 
UPWARDS 

When the  Department of Energy’s Advanced  Research  Projects Agency— 
Energy launched the Optimizing Nuclear Waste and Advanced  Reactor 
Disposal Systems (ONWARDS) program in 2022, it posed a  challenge 

that the nuclear industry had  never seriously confronted before:  how to 
design waste management solutions that anticipate the  coming shift to 
advanced reactors and  not merely retrofit existing systems built for an 
older generation of technology. 

Go  deeper on Nuclear NewsWire. 

TBT:  Celebrating  Seaborg 

Leslie Ayers  (standing  at  right),  then  chairman  of  the  Washington  D.C. ANS  Local 

Section,  presenting  Helen  Seaborg  and  her  husband,  Glenn,  with  certificates  of 

appreciation  at  a  Society  dinner  meeting.  (Photo:  NN,  Sept.  1970,  p.  72) 

Any of list  of history’s  most  notable  nuclear figures would  certainly be 

incomplete without  Glenn  Seaborg,  who was  a  Fellow of the American 

Nuclear Society and  a  member for 43  years. 

Seaborg is  perhaps  best  known for the  1941  experiment where  he  and 

several partners  bombarded uranium with deuterons  using  a  60-inch 

cyclotron,  isolating  an  unstable  element with 94  protons  in  its  nucleus. 

He later proposed to  name this  newly discovered element  after the planet 

Pluto,  dubbing it plutonium. 

Seaborg is  also  credited with the discovery of nine other transuranic 

elements:  americium, berkelium,  californium, curium,  einsteinium, 

fermium, mendelevium, nobelium,  and  seaborgium. 

Aside from his many scientific contributions to the  nuclear world, 

Seaborg was  also  a profoundly influential political voice.  He  served as 

chairman of the Atomic Energy Commission under  Kennedy, Johnson, 

and Nixon and was a  science  advisor to  no fewer than ten presidents. 

He spoke frequently on the value  of nuclear power. One quote  published 

in a brief Nuclear News biography provides his insights  on  nuclear 

energy’s value for the environment: 

An environmentalist myself,  I  remain convinced that nuclear power 

remains the best choice—and I  believe that  in the future most 

environmentalists will come  around to support it.  The world is 

going to  need huge  amounts  of energy if the population in the 

developing countries  is going to  enjoy the  standard of living we do. 

Nuclear power's  pollution and  safety record is  at least as  good  as 

that of other major sources. The cost of burning coal  includes the 

deaths  of miners,  strip mines that poison  streams,  and  acid rain. 

More radiation is  released into the  atmosphere from a  coal-burning 

plant than from a  nuclear plant.  Our reliance on oil has led to  Exxon 

Valdez-—sized oil spills,  acid rain, and our entanglement in the 

Mideast—where hundreds  of thousands  of people were killed and 

tremendous amounts of pollution released in  Desert  Storm.  Ifa 

utility tried to build a  solar electricity plant large enough to power a 

city, the facility would cover so  much  acreage that environmentalists 

would oppose it, just as  they are opposing wind farm  construction 

today. 

Go deeper:  Read more of Seaborg’s story in the April 1999  issue  of 

Nuclear News (pp.  64-66).  Gnembers only) 

Our energy future is nuclear. 
Paragon is proud to power it.

In  quickest  review,  NRC  approves 20- 
year  renewal  for  Robinson 

The Nuclear  Regulatory Commission  has  renewed the  Robinson nuclear 
power plant’s operating license  in  record time.  The subsequent license 
renewal process  for the facility was  completed within  12  months. The 
process has  typically taken  18  months. 

Go  deeper on  Nuclear Newswire.

called the Nuclear Dominance—3  by 33  campaign.

Missouri  News 

Missouri  House  passes  second  attempt  at  legislation  changing 

property tax  policies 
Missouri  House  members  have  passed  legislation  aimed  at  lowering  property 

taxes. 

Representatives  voted  133-13  to  pass  the  legislation.  It  now  goes  to  the 

Senate,  where  Republican  Senate  Floor  Leader  Tony  Luetkemeyer, R- 

Parkville,  has  already said  reducing  property  taxes  is  a  priority. 

Speaking  before  the  vote,  bill  sponsor  Rep.  Tim  Taylor, R-Bunceton,  praised 

the  legislation  and  the  work  of  the  special  committee  he  chaired  on  property 

taxes. 

“If this  passes  this  final journey,  we  can  assure  our  constituents  that  we  have 
made  their  lives  better, and,  quite  frankly,  made  our  lives  better  as 
taxpayers  as  well,  in  stabilizing  the  current  system  so  that we  can  continue 
to  work  forward  in  the years  to  come  to  make  it  even  better,” Taylor said.

A ballot  measure  pushed  by  Republicans  to  change  how  majorities  are  calculated  in  Missouri  for 
constitutional  amendments  proposed  by  initiative  must  clearly  alert  voters  that  is  its  intent,  a  Cole 
County judge  ruled  Wednesday.  Read  more

signatures  for  a  November  referendum.  Read  more

line  committee  vote Wednesday.  Read  more

in  state  general  revenue  —  to  help  the  state  implement  federally  mandated  changes  to  safety  net 
programs.  Read  more

Don  Mayhew  believes  in  the  free  market.  “We  have  choice  in  a  lot  of  different  things.  Go  to  a  grocery 

store  —  there’s four  different  brands  of  green  beans  you  can  buy,”  said  Mayhew,  a  Republican  Crocker. 

“We  know  that  competition  in  any  marketplace  is  the  foundation  —  the  cornerstone  —  of  our  capitalist 
system.”  Read  more

platform  is  late  and  over  budget  —  and  the  program’s top  executive  can’t say  when  it will  be  working  or 
how  much  more  it  will  cost.  Read  more

create an  optional  agriculture education  program  for  elementary  schools.  Read  more 

County  News 

‘Blinded  by  dollar  signs’:  Independence  wrestles with 
90%  tax abatement  for  massive Al  data  center 
Rachel  Gonzalez  did  not  foresee  becoming  an  opposition  leader  to 
what  could  be  the  largest  economic  development  project  in  the 
history  of  Independence,  Missouri. 

She  is  a  lifelong  resident,  living  just  a  few miles  from  a  proposed 
hyperscale  data  center  site.  She  knows  and  voted  for  several of  the 
council  members  who  will  decide  whether  to  give  tax  abatements 
to  the  project  —  a  vote  that  may  ultimately  decide  the  fate  of  the 
“Al factory.” 

When  she  first  heard  about  the  data  center  late  last  year, she 

assumed  the  more  she  learned,  the  better  she  would  feel  about  it. 

The  opposite  happened.  Read  more 

Property tax  caps  on  April  ballot  in  several  counties  worry  districts 

At  the April  7  election,  voters  in  several  southwest  Missouri  counties  will  be  asked  to  weigh  in  on  property 

tax  caps  created  by  state  legislators.  As  assessments  and  property  tax  bills  have  balloons,  limiting  how 

much  property  taxes  can  grow in  the  future  could  be  enticing,  but  the  constitutionality  of  the  legislation 

is  yet  to  be  decided  and  the  tax  freezes  could  have  significant  effects  on  the  services  property  taxes 

fund.  Read  more 

Boone  County  considers  ending  renewal  requirement for  senior  property tax  program 
The  Boone  County  Commission  is  considering  an  amendment  to  the  county's  senior  real  estate  tax 
program  policy  that  would  remove  the  requirement  for  annual  renewal.  Read  more

tight  budget.  Read  more

A Festus  resident  has  filed  a  lawsuit  against  the  city of  Festus,  asking  the  courts  to  reverse  recent  City 
Council  actions  involving  data  centers.  Read  more

Share  your  county's  achievements and  honors 
The Missouri  Association  of Counties  would  like  you  to  Show Me  Your 
County. 

Show Me Your County is a chance for our counties to highlight a 

program,  initiative  or  praise  a  hard-working  county employee  who  has 
gone  above  and  beyond  their  duties  or  has  received  an  award. 

( HOW  ME 

Counties  can  submit  a  500-word  or  less  description  of  the  program, 

initiative or employee, along with pictures if applicable.  Those 
submissions  will  then  be  featured  on  MAC’s social  media  platforms  and 
in  the Missouri  County  Record  magazine. 

(

NACo  partners  with  thought  leaders,  industry  partners  and  philanthropic 
organizations  to  identify  and  elevate  best  practices  in  key  areas  of  county

Communities Committed to

Governor  Kehoe  announces  opportunity  for  more  Missouri 

communities  to  earn  Blue  Shield  designation 
Governor Mike  Kehoe  announced  Missouri  counties,  towns,  and  cities  can  now 

apply  to  be  recognized  as  Blue  Shield  communities  in  2026  for  their dedication 

to  effective  law  enforcement  and  community  safety. 

The Missouri  Department  of  Public  Safety  administers  the  Blue  Shield  Program. 

Applications  should  be  made  by  an  ofÏcial  from  the  jurisdictions  seeking  the 
Blue  Shield  designation  in  coordination  with  the  jurisdiction’s chief  law 

enforcement  ofÏcer. 

Deadline  to  submit  is  June  1,  2026.  Applications  are  made  online,  and  all 
supporting  materials  can  be  uploaded  here  .

Subject: Tax Revenue  Estimates 

Hi  Callaway  County  Commissioners  - 

|  hope  you  all  had  a  nice  week.  Please  find  attached  the  updated  tax  revenue estimates  broken  out  by 

each  taxing  jurisdiction.  These  estimates  are  based  on  the  requested  terms:  an  85%  property tax 

abatement  for  a  25-year term,  assuming  the  Ambulance  and  Fire  District  opts  out  of the  Chapter  100 

abatement.  This  will  result  in  an  estimated  438%  annual  increase  to  the  School  District's  revenue  and  a 

31%  annual  increase to  the  County's  revenue. 

Both  attached  files  contain  the  same  numbers,  just formatted  differently. We  look forward  to 

discussing  this  proposal  in  further  detail  on  Monday  and  reviewing  the  potential  community  benefit 

projects  you  all  provide. 

Disclaimer: All tax  revenue  figures presented in  this proposal are  estimates  only and are  subject to  change 

depending  on  market conditions,  the  requirements of the  end-user tenant,  the  final design  and  configuration  of the 

facility,  the  total capital investment deployed,  and the  application  of Callaway County Assessor rules  and 

assessment practices. 

Thanks

From:  Grimm,  Mark  (G&B)  <MGRIMM@GilmoreBell.com>

Subject:  Crusoe  Project 

Gary, 

Attached  are estimates  of  real  property taxes for the  proposed  data  center.  One  big  uncertainty  is  the 

valuation  of the  data  center.  In  Montgomery  County, Amazon  estimated  a  value  equal  to  45%  of the  real 

property  cost.  That  same  valuation  was  used  in  Warrenton/Crusoe —  though  |  don’t  recall  any  specific 

discussions  about that  point  with  Crusoe  or  its  lawyers  (Polsinelli).  For the  Montgomery  County/Google 
data  center, Google  (through  Polsinelli)  is  asking  us  to  use  a  value  of $300/sf.  Travis thinks  that  is 

ridiculously  low.  One  of my  partners  had  discussions with  the  Clay  County Assessor regarding  valuation 
of a  data  center  in  Liberty,  and  the Assessor estimated  $436/sf. 

So  bottom  line:  we've  shown  all  three  scenarios;  as you  can  see,  they  produce very  different  results. 

Please  call  if you'd like  to  discuss.

Christopher  Scott 
Conor  Bates-Janigo;  Curt Warfield;  Gary Jungermann 
Re:  Post-Discussion  questions  from  today 

Good  afternoon,  Commissioner Scott. Thank you for continuing to field the  public’s questions.  Please 
see  answers below in  red. 

Have a  nice weekend. 

Heather Tazalla 
htazalla@crusoe.ai  |  214-263-8388 

On  Fri,  Jul 17,  2026 at  12:01  PM  Christopher Scott <cscott@callawaycounty.org> wrote: 

Heather, Conor, 

|  had  a  good  discussion with  Danielle  Lawson and  Jennifer Love today (FB:  Callaway Data Watch).  It was 

positive.  Danielle expressed that the  open  house answered  most of her questions.  She did  say that she 

still has some questions  not answered or feels that she  received  answers that differed from what she felt 

like  she  heard from other community discussions. 

Below are a  couple topics on which  she  said  she would  like  more detail: 

1.  Site studies 

a. 

She asked  if there was a  "Heritage  Review"  being performed.  She  referenced the  MO  Dept.  of 

Conservation.  Here  is a  link to their site. 

https://naturalheritagereview.mdc.mo.gov/ 

This appears to  be  an  unofÏcial site  review that can  be  performed  online to determine  if the  area  of 

interest contains any plant of animal species considered to  be a  "Sensitive  natural resource".  A full on- 

site  review may be  requested.  Yes, we  completed  this  as  part  of our site  diligence.  Nothing  requiring 

further study was  noted  /  no  sensitive  natural  resources. 

e  Will any of the  site  studies  performed  (environmental, geological,  etc)  be  made  public? We  have 

completed  all of these  reports  (which  inform  both  our  site  design  and  our decision to  move  forward  at  a 

given  site).  However, as  we  do  not own  the  underlying  parcel,  our ability to  access and  complete 

diligence  on  the  site  is  through  an  access agreement with  confidentiality provisions. We therefore are 

unable to  release these  reports  publicly as  it would  put  us  in  breach  of our confidentiality provisions. 

e  Discharge of treated water 

a.  What type(s) of water will be  discharged from the  site? There  are  2  waste  streams:  septic  (only 

from domestic  sinks  and  toilets).  This will be  traditional spray or  leach field.  Cooling 

system:  Collection  and  offsite  disposal.  This does  not discharge to  the  site,  it  is  collected. 

What is the expected discharge of the glycol-mix cooling fluids?  What quantity?  What frequency? 
Most weeks, the  glycol mix will  be  0  gal discharge.  Our engineering lead  estimates that  if there  ever were 
repairs  requiring discharge  (possibly every few months),  there  may be  a  collection of 500 -  2,000 gallons. 
This  really depends  on  operations and  maintenance  requirements. 
e  How will discharged fluids  be treated?  Where will they be treated? 
The  cooling system fluids will be  collected  by vacuum truck and  hauled to  a  qualified  disposal facility ina 
nearby city. The  glycol mix will never be  sent to  storm,  or septic,  or disposed  onsite  by any other means 

Thank you for your continued  cooperation. 

Christopher Scott

HALF  OF  THE NORTHEAST QUARTER OF  THE  SOUTHEAST QUARTER OF  SECTION 

THIRTY-SIX  (36),  TOWNSHIP FORTY-NINE (49),  RANGE  TEN  (10),  CALLAWAY COUNTY,

CRUSOE TECHNOLOGIES 
CONFIDENTIAL 

May  8th,  2026 

Crusoe 

Re:  Proposed  Chapter  100  Industrial  Development  Revenue  Bond  Agreement —  Crusoe 
Technologies Data  Center  Project,  Callaway  County,  Missouri 

Dear  Callaway  County  Commissioners: 

Crusoe  Technologies  LLC  (“Crusoe”)  is  pleased  to  present this  formal  request for  a  Chapter  100 
Industrial  Development  Revenue  Bond  (“Chapter  100”)  agreement  with  Callaway  County, 
Missouri.  Crusoe  is  a  technology  infrastructure  company  focused  on  purpose-built,  sustainable 
data  centers  and  high-performance  computing  facilities.  We  are  actively  evaluating  Callaway 
County  as  the  site  for  a  significant  capital  investment  that  would  bring  lasting  economic  and 
fiscal  benefits  to  the  region. 

We  respectfully  propose  the  following  terms  and  set forth  below  our  substantive justifications  for 
those  terms,  along  with  a  summary  of the  projected  fiscal  impact  (“85%  PDF’). 

Il.  PROPOSED  CHAPTER  100  TERMS 

Agreement Term: 25  years  from  the  date  of  project  completion. 

Abatement  Percentage:  85%  abatement  on  real  property  and  personal  property taxes 

assessed  against the  project  during  the  agreement term. 

Property  Subject to  Abatement: All  real  property  comprising  the  data  center  facility  and 

associated  on-site  improvements,  and  all  personal  property,  including  servers,  networking 

equipment,  and  other  qualifying  tangible  personal  property  placed  in  service  at  the  site. 

PILOT &  Community  Benefit  Payments:  Payments  in  Lieu  of Taxes (“PILOT”)  and  Community 

Benefit  payments  will  be  structured  in  collaboration  with  the  taxing  jurisdictions  to  direct 

meaningful,  recurring  revenue to  the  community. 

Jobs Commitment:  Crusoe  shall  commit  to  creating  and  maintaining  an  agreed  upon  minimum 

number  of full-time  equivalent jobs  at  the  facility,  with  an  average  annual  wage  equal  to  or 

exceeding  150%  of the  average  annual  wage  in  Callaway  County,  as  determined  by  reference 
to  applicable  Missouri  Department  of  Economic  Development data. 

Fees:  Crusoe  shall  be  responsible for  reasonable  and  documented  legal  fees  and  expenses  of 
the  County  related  to  the  Project,  including  Gilmore  &  Bell,  P.C., bond  counsel  to  the  County, 
and  the  selected  bond  trustee. 

ll.  PROJECTED  FISCAL  IMPACT 

The  following  table  summarizes  the  projected  tax  revenue to  local  taxing  jurisdictions,  based  on 

the  estimated  assessed  value  of  the  project.  These  figures  will  flow  directly  to  local  taxing 
jurisdictions  each  year,  plus  any  Community  Benefit  payments  negotiated  as  part  of the  Chapter 

100  structure.  The figures  presented  below  are  preliminary,  illustrative  estimates  only  and  do  not 

constitute  a  representation,  warranty,  or  commitment  by  Crusoe  as  to  actual  tax  revenue  that 
will  be  generated  by  the  project.  These  estimates  are  based  on  assumptions  regarding  capital 

investment,  facility  configuration,  end-user  tenant  requirements,  and  assessment  methodology, 

any  of  which  may  change.  Actual  results  may  differ  materially.  Please  review  the  disclaimer 

following  the  table  for  additional  detail. 

Crusoe  Energy  Systems  |  Chapter  100  Proposal  |  Callaway  County.  MissouriPage  1

Disclaimer:  All  tax  revenue  figures  presented  in  this  proposal  are  estimates  only  and  are  subject  to 
change  depending  on  market  conditions,  the  requirements  of  the  end-user  tenant.  the  final  design  and 
configuration  of  the  facility,  the  total  capital  investment  deployed,  and  the  application  of Callaway  County 
Assessor rules  and  assessment practices. 

lll.  JUSTIFICATION  FOR  PROPOSED TERMS 

Crusoe  respectfully  submits  that  the  proposed  25-year  term  and  85%  abatement  are 
well-supported  by  the  specific  economic  and  development  characteristics  of this  project  and  the 
Callaway  County  market.  The  following  factors,  taken  together, distinguish  this  project from  sites 
in  competing  jurisdictions: 

1.  Higher  Property  Tax Rate  Than  Comparable  Sites 

Callaway  County’s  effective  property  tax  rate  on  commercial  and  industrial  real  and  personal 

property 

is  materially  higher 

than 

that  of  comparable  data  center  sites  under  active 

consideration  in  Missouri  and  the  nation.  The  85%  abatement  corrects  for  this  structural  cost 

disparity  and  places  the  County  on  an  equal  footing  with  competing  sites. 

2.  Hyperscaler Tenant Requirements  &  Site  Competitiveness 

Crusoe  is  pursuing  a  hyperscaler  tenant  for  this  facility.  The  tenant  will  be  a  large-scale, 

credit-worthy  technology  company  with  the  financial  capacity  to  make  substantial  ongoing 

investments  in  GPU  and  computing  infrastructure  at  the  site.  Hyperscaler  tenants  operate  under 

rigorous’ 

internal 

return-on-capital 

frameworks  and  evaluate  competing 

sites  on 

a 

total-cost-of-ownership  basis.  An  abatement  structure  that  keeps  the  all-in  occupancy  cost 

competitive  is  often  a  threshold  requirement  for  site  selection.  Securing  such  a  tenant  is  critical 

not  only  to  the  viability  of  this  project,  but  to  maximizing  the  long-term  capital  investment  and 

associated  economic  activity  in  Callaway  County. 

3.  Higher  Utility  &  Transmission Costs 

Data  center  operations  are  energy-intensive.  The  utility  and  transmission  cost  structure  for  this 

prospective  project  is  higher  than  in  certain  competing  markets,  adding  meaningful  operating 

cost  pressure to  the  project on  an  annual  basis. 

4.  Higher  Public  Improvement Costs 

Development  of  this  site  will  require  significant  public  infrastructure  improvements.  These 

include  road  improvements  to  accommodate  the  construction  trafÏc  and  ongoing  operational 
logistics  associated  with  a  data  center  campus,  as  well  as  a  necessary  waterline  upgrade  to 
ensure  adequate  water  supply  and  pressure  for  cooling  and  fire  suppression  systems.  These 
capital  costs,  borne  directly  by  Crusoe,  represent a  real  and  material  cost to  develop  the  project. 

Crusoe  Energy  Systems  |  Chapter  100  Proposal  |  Callaway  County,  MissouriPage  2 

CRUSOE  TECHNOLOGIES 

CONFIDENTIAL 

Crusoe  : 

5.  Structuring  Payments  to  Keep  Money  Local 

The  Chapter  100  structure  affords  considerable  flexibility  in  how  PILOT and  Community  Benefit 
payments  are  allocated  and  directed.  Crusoe  is  committed  to  working  collaboratively  with 
Callaway  County  and  the  other  taxing  jurisdictions  to  design  a  payment  structure  that  is 
consistent  with  applicable  law  and  delivers  meaningful,  recurring  revenue to  local  entities. 

6. 

Comparable  Data  Center  Chapter  100  Agreement terms  in  Missouri 

Crusoe  believes  the  proposed  abatement  terms  are  more  competitive  and  result  in  higher 

community  benefit than  other  recently  approved  Chapter  100  Bond  Agreements.  For  example: 

e  Nebius  —  Independence,  Missouri  (Jackson  County)  Approved  March  2026 

o  20-year  term  at  98%  abatement  for  both  real  and  90%  abatement  for  personal 

property taxes 

e  Mica —  Kansas  City,  Missouri  (Port  KC) Approved  in  July  2025 

o  25-year  term  with  a  75%  abatement  on  real  and  personal  property  taxes, 

complemented  by  a  100%  sales  tax  exemption  for  all  construction  materials  and 

project  equipment. 

IV.  NEXT  STEPS 

Crusoe  welcomes  the  opportunity  to  present  these  terms  in  greater  detail  to  the  Commission 

and  its  advisors  and  to  work  cooperatively  toward  a  mutually  beneficial  agreement.  We  are 

prepared  to  engage  directly  with  any  relevant  stakeholders  to  advance  this  process  and  work 

collaboratively with  the  community. 

We  look  forward  to  a  continued  and  productive  dialogue  regarding  this  important  investment 
opportunity  for  Callaway  County.  Please  do  not  hesitate  to  contact  us  at  your  earliest 
convenience. 

Respectfully  submitted, 

Crusoe Technologies LLC 

Lucas Workman 

State  and  Local  Economic  Development

Gary Jungermann;  Callaway Commission;  Heather Tazalla; Conor  Bates-Janigo 

Tax Revenue  Estimates 

85%  Public.pdf;  85%  PublicV2.pdf 

Hi  Callaway County Commissioners - 

|  hope you  all had  a  nice week.  Please find  attached the  updated tax  revenue estimates  broken out  by 

each taxing  jurisdiction. These estimates are  based  on the  requested terms:  an  85%  property tax 

abatement for a  25-year term,  assuming the Ambulance  and  Fire  District opts  out of the  Chapter 100 

abatement. This will result in  an  estimated 438% annual increase to the School District's  revenue and  a 

31% annual increase to the  County's  revenue. 

Both  attached files contain the  same  numbers, just formatted  differently. We look forward  to  discussing 

this  proposal in  further detail on  Monday and  reviewing the  potential community benefit  projects you  all 

provide. 

Disclaimer:  All tax revenue  figures presented  in  this  proposal are  estimates  only  and  are  subject to  change  depending  on 

market  conditions,  the  requirements  of the  end-user tenant,  the  final  design  and  configuration  of the  facility,  the  total 

capital investment  deployed,  and  the  application  of Callaway  County Assessor rules  and  assessment practices. 

Thanks 

Lucas Workman 

Manager, State  &  Local  Economic  Development 

Phone  Number:  (970)  302-1879
